Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. I'm mostly just here because... well, for a long time now, my sleep has been a little broken.

Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tired during the day. I mean, I try not to let it show because I know my clients rely on me, and I want to do a good job for them.

And then I'm always up way before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really rested at all. \n\nI hope it's okay that I came in to ask about that. I just wanted to see if maybe I was doing something wrong? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if it’s usually hard for you to fall asleep at the beginning of the night, or do you feel like you fall asleep pretty easily but then wake up later?\n\nPatient: Oh, right, I'm sorry if I didn't explain that very well. Um, falling asleep is actually the easy part. I really do just drop right off the second my head hits the pillow because I'm so worn out from work. \n\nIt's just the rest of the night that's the trouble. I'll just pop awake at the slightest little noise, and then... well, then I'm just lying there way before my alarm is supposed to go off, feeling like I barely slept. I really hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it, but... yes, it's definitely more the waking up later that happens to me. Did I answer that right? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: How often does this trouble with waking up during the night and not being able to go back to sleep happen? Is it at least a few times a week, and has it been going on for a month or more?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, it's definitely a few times a week. Almost every night, really, but I try to just push through it so I don't bother anyone. \n\nAs for how long... well, it's been quite a while. I hope you don't think I'm silly for letting it go on this long before coming in. It's actually been a little over two years now. It started right around the time my mother-in-law moved in with us. We just wanted to make sure she was taken care of, you know? So I didn't want to complain about my sleep when she needed help. \n\nIs it okay that I waited this long to ask about it? I just didn't want to be a bother.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired or not well-rested during the day because of your sleep difficulties?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tired during the day. I mean, I try not to let it show because I know my clients rely on me, and I want to do a good job for them. \n\nBut... by the time I finish seeing my home-health clients and come back to help out at home, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing the little things, like charting my notes or folding a basket of laundry, it can feel like... like it's almost too much to push through. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm just being lazy or complaining too much. I really do try to keep up with everything. Is it normal to feel that worn out, or am I just not managing my time right?\n\nDoctor: Even if you fall asleep easily at the beginning of the night, do you often feel like your sleep isn’t refreshing, like you’re not getting the rest your body needs?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I think that's exactly it. Like I was trying to say earlier—and I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself too much—my sleep just feels so... broken. I'll fall into bed completely exhausted, but because I wake up to every little creak in the house and end up awake way before my alarm, I just... I wake up feeling like I never really rested at all. \n\nI mean, I know I'm getting some sleep, so I really shouldn't complain, but it just doesn't feel like it's doing any good. It really does feel like my body just isn't getting the rest it needs to keep going. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep through the night or get a full amount of sleep, do you still wake up feeling unrefreshed or like your sleep wasn’t restful?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... you know, I'm actually not entirely sure. It's just that... well, I really don't sleep all the way through the night very often anymore, so it's hard to compare. \n\nI think... I think if I actually did manage to get a full, uninterrupted night of sleep, I'd probably feel okay in the morning? I just haven't really had the chance to notice lately since I'm always waking up in the middle of it. I'm so sorry, I wish I could give you a more helpful answer. I hope it's okay that I don't really know for sure?\n\nDoctor: Even if you do manage to get a full or uninterrupted night of sleep, do you still wake up feeling like you didn’t get enough rest or that your sleep wasn’t restful?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... well, I know you just asked about that, and I'm really sorry, I don't mean to be difficult or anything. It's just... like I said, I really don't get a full night's sleep very often anymore, so it's hard to say for sure. \n\nI really want to give you a good answer, but I just haven't had a full night of sleep lately to even know if I'd feel rested or not. I'm sorry, I know that's not very helpful. I hope it's alright that I'm just not sure?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when you felt like you slept through the night without waking up, and if so, did you wake up feeling refreshed or still tired?\n\nPatient: Oh dear... I feel terrible because I know we keep circling back to this, and I really, really wish I could give you the answer you're looking for. I feel like I must be doing a bad job explaining things, and I'm so sorry. \n\nBut honestly, no... there just haven't been any times recently where I actually slept all the way through the night without waking up. Not that I can remember, anyway. I'm usually up at least once or twice, checking the clock or just lying there. So I really can't say if I'd wake up tired or refreshed after a solid night, because it just hasn't happened in a while. \n\nI hope I'm not frustrating you... I'm really trying to think of a time so I can answer your question properly, but my sleep has just been so choppy. Is it okay that I just don't have a good example for you?\n\nDoctor: Even when you feel like you’ve had enough sleep, do you still wake up feeling tired or like your sleep wasn’t enough to make you feel ready for the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I feel awful, I know you're trying so hard to get a sense of this, and I keep giving you the same answer. I really don't mean to be a bother or make your job difficult. \n\nIt's just... I honestly can't even remember the last time I felt like I got *enough* sleep, you know? It's always so broken up. So I just don't have a good gauge on whether I'd feel ready for the day if I actually got a full, good night's rest. I'd like to think I would! I really don't want to be a difficult patient for you, I promise. \n\nIt's just... I really want to give you a proper answer about those peaceful, undisturbed nights, but I just don't have them. I just really want to make sure I'm being a good patient and giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a night where you didn’t wake up at all and felt like you had plenty of time to sleep, and if so, did you wake up feeling refreshed or still tired?\n\nPatient: Oh, doctor... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... I see you had to ask me the exact same thing again. I feel just sick about it, I really do. I must be making your job so hard today, and I am so, so sorry. 

I'm sitting here racking my brain, trying to find just one single night to tell you about so you can check that box on your form. I really, really want to give you what you need. But... I just can't think of a time where I slept all the way through like that. Not in a very, very long time. I guess if I did, I would hope I'd feel refreshed? But I honestly just don't know for sure because it hasn't happened. 

I'm failing this part of the interview, aren't I? I feel terrible that I keep stalling us on this one question and giving you nothing to work with. Please tell me I'm not ruining your schedule... I promise I'm trying my absolute best for you.
